❯ explain me the agreed upon sentiment where is it in the project file          

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ It's in the brief, five-sites-by-thursday.md:53, under the heading "The 
  scoring rubric we agreed with the sponsor". Sentiment is one row of that
  table, C4, at line 60.

  Bubble analogy: the rubric is like an option set that the client signed off
  on. You can use it in your workflows, but you don't edit its values on your
  own. The sponsor agreed to these criteria and weights, so if we change them,
  the sponsor is shown a score they never agreed to. That's why the assumption
  under Q1 says "keep the weights exactly as they are".

  The rubric: 6 criteria, weights add up to 100

  ┌─────┬───────────────────────────────┬────────┐
  │  #  │           Criterion           │ Weight │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C1  │ Grid headroom                 │ 30     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C2  │ Distance to substation        │ 15     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C3  │ Landowner status              │ 20     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C4  │ Council / community sentiment │ 10     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C5  │ Flood zone                    │ 15     │
  ├─────┼───────────────────────────────┼────────┤
  │ C6  │ Usable area                   │ 10     │
  └─────┴───────────────────────────────┴────────┘

  C4 in detail (line 60): supportive scores 5, neutral 3, opposed 0.

  Each criterion adds weight × tier ÷ 5 to the total. For a supportive site, C4
  adds 10 × 5 ÷ 5 = 10 points. Neutral adds 6, opposed adds 0. So sentiment is
  worth at most 10 of the 100 points.

  Where sentiment lives in the data: only in the free-text field notes. The data
  dictionary says so (data/DATA_DICTIONARY.md:56): "Community sentiment is only
  ever in the free text." There's no structured field for it. That's why Claude
  has to read the notes. Real examples from the data:
  - S-003: "Council planning committee minutes record support for local storage
    on this plot." Supportive, 5.
  - S-010: "Council has no formal position yet; officer said 'depends on the
    noise report'." Neutral, 3.
  - S-030 has a petition against the project. Opposed, 0.

  Who does what (N8N analogy): Claude is the AI node. It reads each note and
  returns sentiment: supportive along with the exact quote. Then rank.py is the
  Code node that turns "supportive" into 5 and then into 10 points. The brief
  requires that split: "Claude reads the notes; your code decides the scores."

  Next: say "go step 2" to start rank.py, or ask about any other rubric row.

  Based on your request and our discussion, here's the scope I'm proposing to
  plan against:

  The plan covers everything after QUESTIONS.md:
  - One-command Python script. Loads data/, fixes the data traps, has Claude
    read each site's notes (with verified quotes), scores in code against the
    sponsor rubric, and writes a static phone page.
  - Extraction check. A hand-labelled sample compared against Claude's output,
    with the result in EVAL.md.
  - Docs. README.md, NOTE.md, AI_LOG.md, and the transcripts export.
  - Deploy of the static page.

  Out of scope: any database, an agent framework, CI, tests beyond the
  extraction check, and design polish.

  Carrying forward (your decisions, not reopened):
  - Rubric weights: exactly as agreed with the sponsor. You chose this over
    re-weighting from your own research.
  - Missing values: score 0 and are marked "unknown". You chose this over
    dataset averaging.
  - Vendor headroom: used only when gridmap has no figure, at the low end of the
    vendor's band, and flagged "unverified". You chose this over a 1:1 fallback
    and over dropping a tier.
  - Kill rule: applies if the land registry or a field note says protected area.
    You chose this over registry only.
  - Nordholm kW to MW (÷ 1,000): done in the scoring script. You chose this over
    fixing the extraction job.
  - Not-contacted landowners: kept, scored 1 out of 5 by the rubric. You chose
    this over excluding them.
  - No database: scratch.db stays a local viewer. You chose this over SQLite.

  Call outs:
  - The clock. About 2h of the 4h cap is left, so I'd shrink the gold set to
    about 8 sites (the trap sites) and ship the page before any polish. The
    alternative is PLAN.md's 12 sites and risking going over 4h.
  - Hosting. The repo is private, so GitHub Pages isn't free. I'd deploy only
    the generated page folder to Vercel instead.
  - Best-case score. Your rule makes missing = 0, and I'd rank by that. I'd also
    show a "best case if unknowns check out" score, so strong-but-unverified
    sites like S-024 are visible. The alternative is showing the 0-based score
    only.

⏺ Plan written to /Users/prab/Downloads/assignment-venture-engineer/docs/plans/2
  026-09-25-001-feat-notes-extraction-plan.md. I read all 70 notes to build it.

  What the notes table showed
  - Only 18 different sentences across 70 notes. The business development team
    reused stock phrases. Bubble analogy: it's like an option set disguised as
    free text.
  - Owner status (C3) is already a structured field on almost every owner note.
    Only S-024 (no notes) and S-036 ("No idea who owns it") have none. So the
    proposal is: the structured field decides C3, and Claude double-checks it.
  - Sentiment (C4) is free text only. That's where Claude does the real work.
    Seven phrasings sort into supportive, neutral or opposed. 12 sites have no
    sentiment note, so they get unknown, which scores 0 under your rule.
  - Three facts exist only in the notes, and each one changes the result:
    - S-009 is in a protected reserve, so it's excluded.
    - S-013 has shrunk to 700 m², so it scores 0 on area.
    - S-020's latest note is "refused". Its notes are out of date order, so
      reading them top to bottom gets this wrong.
  - Caveats to show on the page, with no score change: S-003 wants a 12% revenue
    share, and S-020 says "revisit in 2027".

  The AI design (N8N analogy: AI node, then Code node)
  1. One Claude call per site. Claude gets that site's notes, sorted by date,
     and returns JSON: owner, sentiment, area, protected, caveats. Each comes
     with its exact quote, or "unknown".
  2. Code checks that every quote really appears in the notes. A made-up quote
     gets thrown out, and that signal becomes "unknown".
  3. Code, not Claude, picks the latest note for each signal and turns it into
     rubric points. Claude never sees the rubric.
  4. Checking it: I hand-label the 18 distinct sentences once and copy those
     labels to every site. That gives a correct answer for all 39 sites with
     notes, a full check instead of a sample, for about 15 minutes of labelling.

  One decision for you: S-036 says "No idea who owns it". I defaulted it to
  owner unknown, which scores 0. The alternative is not contacted, which scores
  1.

  Skipped for time: I didn't run the research subagents (there's no code yet to
  research) or the automatic doc review. Either can still run if you want them.

❯ ponytail ultra how is the plan looking?                                       

⏺ The plan is committed (2b5ee08), with all 4 reviewer decisions applied. It's
  sound: feasibility checked every fact against the data. On the ultra setting,
  five things can still be cut:

  1. Drop note_date from what Claude returns. Code already knows which note a
     quote came from, because the quote has to match a note, and so it knows the
     date. Asking Claude for the date again adds a way for it to be wrong and
     gives us nothing new.
  2. Drop the 5 caveat kinds (commercial, timing, …). Nothing reads the kind;
     the page just shows the text. Keep only {detail, quote}.
  3. Drop the separate out/rejected_quotes.json. Record a rejected quote inside
     the site's entry in extractions.json instead.
  4. Replace check_extraction.py with rank.py --check. One script. The labels
     (eval/gold_notes.json) and EVAL.md stay.
  5. Replace the prompt-hash cache key with --refresh. You're the only one
     editing the prompt. Borderline: it saves about 3 lines. I'd cut it.

  Keep everything else:
  - One call per site
  - Quote verification
  - Latest-note-wins rule
  - Both owner values stored side by side
  - source on the protected-area flag
  - The full 39-site check

  Net effect: 2 fewer files, a smaller schema, and one less thing Claude can get
  wrong.

  Next: say "cut all 5", or name the numbers to keep, and I'll update the plan,
  commit it, then start step 2 (the data layer).

      69  - **KTD8. Structured output via a forced tool call with a JSON 
          schema.** Enums include `unknown`. `claude-opus-5` by default, 
          with a `MODEL` env override.
      70  - **KTD9. The check uses gold labels per distinct note text, ex
          panded to every site.** There are only 18 distinct texts, so la
          belling those by hand gives a gold answer for **all 39 sites wi
          th notes**. That's a full check, not a sample, for about 15 min
          utes of labelling.

      105 -**Approach:** The prompt defines each category in plain words

          -verbatim, one continuous span", "unknown if not stated" and "d
          -o not infer from tone". It gives no rubric numbers. The site's
          - notes go in date-sorted, each tagged with date. The cache is 
          -keyed by site_id plus a hash of the prompt file, so editing th
          -e prompt refreshes results automatically; `--refresh` forces a
          - re-call. The cache is written after each site, so if a call f
          -ails the run stops, and rerunning only calls the API for sites
          - missing from the cache. `.env` is read by hand (no python-dot
          -env), overriding the shell's `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

  - Claude now returns only a value and a quote. Code finds which note the quote

❯ /plan step 2 2A working slice, with Claude in the loop                        
~2 h                                                                            
Something we can run with one command, in any language you like. It should:     
                                                                                
load the data and produce a ranked result Maren could use on Thursday;          
make it possible to see why any site scored the way it did;                     
use Claude to read the field notes. The business development team's notes are   
free text, and some of what the rubric needs only lives there. Call the         
Anthropic API from your script to turn each site's notes into structured        
signals: at least owner status, community sentiment, and anything in the notes  
that should change how the site is scored.                                      
RULES FOR THE CLAUDE STEP                                                       
                                                                                
Every extracted signal carries the exact quote it came from. If the notes don't 
say, the answer is "unknown". Don't let the model guess.                        
Check it. Show us how you know the extraction is right. Hand-checking a sample  
of sites against the model's output and reporting what you found is plenty.     
Claude reads the notes; your code decides the scores. Keep the rubric in your   
code, not in a prompt.                                                          
Keep your prompt(s) in the repo. Use any current Claude model; claude-opus-5 is 
a good default.                                                                 
You don't need an agent framework. One well-designed call per site (or per      
batch) is enough, and choosing the simplest thing that works is part of the     
test.                                                                           
                                                                                
Add a short README.md explaining how to run it and what decisions you made

     │   vendor-lowband | landreg | note | typed-field | unknown), and quote   │
     │   if any. That per-criterion record is the "why".                       │
     │ - Ranking: sort by score, then fewer unknowns. Top 5 are the shortlist. │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Output                                                               │
     │ - Terminal: a ranked table with score, best case, confidence ("n/6      │
     │   known"), flags, and the excluded list with reasons.                   │
     │ - python3 rank.py S-013 prints one site's full breakdown: each          │
     │   criterion with value, source, tier, points and quote. This is the     │
     │   "why" for any site.                                                   │
     │ - Writes out/results.json for the Part 3 page.                          │
     │ - Asserts on every run:                                                 │
     │   - 39 sites after the merge                                            │
     │   - S-020 is refused                                                    │
     │   - S-013 area is 700                                                   │
     │   - S-009 is excluded with source note                                  │
     │   - S-033 is excluded with source registry                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. --check. Load eval/gold_notes.json (18 distinct texts labelled by    │
     │ hand), expand the labels to every site by matching note text, and       │
     │ resolve with the same latest-note logic. Compare against Claude's       │
     │ output per signal, then print agreement and each mismatch with its      │
     │ quote. The result goes into EVAL.md.                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ README.md (short)                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Run: python3 rank.py (cached, no key needed), python3 rank.py         │
     │   --refresh (needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY), python3 rank.py S-0xx, python3   │
     │   rank.py --check.                                                      │
     │ - Decisions: a list with one line each, linking to QUESTIONS.md for the │
     │   reasoning. Covers:                                                    │
     │   - the traps and how each is handled                                   │
     │   - Claude reads, code scores                                           │
     │   - missing = 0 plus best case                                          │
     │   - vendor low-band                                                     │
     │   - the tier boundary choice                                            │
     │   - the merged duplicate                                                │
     │ - Time spent: filled in at the end.                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Commits (in order, each reviewed by user)                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Data layer + rubric scoring (runs with no Claude yet: notes-based    │
     │    criteria unknown).                                                   │

     │ Weight: 10                                                              │
     │ Raw input: Claude sentiment from the notes                              │
     │ Conversion before tiering: quote must match a note                      │
     │ If missing: 0, unknown (12 sites)                                       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: C5                                                                   │
     │ Weight: 15                                                              │
     │ Raw input: landreg.flood_zone                                           │
     │ Conversion before tiering: none                                         │
     │ If missing: 0, unknown (S-024, S-036)                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: C6                                                                   │
     │ Weight: 10                                                              │
     │ Raw input: landreg.area_m2                                              │
     │ Conversion before tiering: replaced by Claude area_override_m2 if the   │
     │ note is                                                                 │
     │ newer than record_date (S-013 becomes 700)                              │
     │ If missing: 0, unknown                                                  │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: Kill                                                                 │
     │ Weight: —                                                               │
     │ Raw input: landreg.protected_area or Claude protected_area              │
     │ Conversion before tiering: —                                            │
     │ If missing: not killed                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Formula check (S-003): C1 4.6 MW → 5 → 30 × 5/5 = 30. C2 0.8 km → 5 →   │
     │ 15. C3 LOI → 5 → 20. C4 supportive → 5 → 10. C5 low → 4 → 15 × 4/5 =    │
     │ 12. C6 2,100 m² → 5 → 10. Total 97. Assert S-003 = 97 in rank.py so the │
     │ formula is checked on every run.                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 5. Output                                                               │
     │ - Terminal: a ranked table with score, best case, confidence ("n/6      │
     │   known"), flags, and the excluded list with reasons.                   │
     │ - python3 rank.py S-013 prints one site's full breakdown: each          │
     │   criterion with value, source, tier, points and quote. This is the     │
     │   "why" for any site.                                                   │
     │ - Writes out/results.json for the Part 3 page.                          │
     │ - Asserts on every run:                                                 │
     │   - 39 sites after the merge                                            │
     │   - S-003 scores 97 (formula check)                                     │
     │   - S-020 is refused                                                    │
     │   - S-013 area is 700                                                   │
     │   - S-009 is excluded with source note                                  │
     │   - S-033 is excluded with source registry                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 6. --check. Load eval/gold_notes.json (18 distinct texts labelled by    │
     │ hand), expand the labels to every site by matching note text, and       │
     │ resolve with the same latest-note logic. Compare against Claude's       │
     │ output per signal, then print agreement and each mismatch with its      │
     │ quote. The result goes into EVAL.md.                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ README.md (short)                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Setup: pip install -r requirements.txt, put the key in .env.          │
     │ - Run: python3 rank.py (cached, no key needed), python3 rank.py         │
     │   --refresh (needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY), python3 rank.py S-0xx, python3   │

     │   reasoning. Covers:                                                    │

     │   - Claude reads, code scores                                           │
     │   - missing = 0 plus best case                                          │

     │   - the merged duplicate                                                │

     │ - C1 headroom: ≥ 4 → 5, ≥ 2 → 3, ≥ 1 → 1, else 0. Boundary: exactly 2.0 │
     │   counts as "2–4", matching how "≥ 4" is written. Stated in the         │
     │   README.                                                               │
     │ - C2 distance: ≤ 1 → 5, ≤ 3 → 3, ≤ 6 → 1, else 0.                       │
     │ - C3 owner: loi_signed 5, in_talks 3, not_contacted 1, refused 0.       │
     │ - C4 sentiment: supportive 5, neutral 3, opposed 0.                     │
     │ - C5 flood: none 5, low 4, medium 2, high 0.                            │
     │ - C6 area: ≥ 1200 → 5, ≥ 800 → 2, else 0.                               │
     │ - Score = Σ weight × tier / 5. Best case = the same sum with unknown    │
     │   tiers set to 5.                                                       │
     │ - Kill: the site is excluded with its reason, source and quote.         │
     │ - Per criterion, each site keeps: tier, raw value, source (gridmap |    │

     │ If missing: not killed                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ C4 community sentiment: how it's derived                                │
     │ 1. Claude gets the site's notes and the definitions below (no points),  │
     │    and returns sentiment: {value, quote}.                               │
     │    - supportive: the council, a councillor, a council committee, or a   │
     │      local group formally backs the project.                            │
     │    - neutral: no position, indifferent, or support conditional on       │
     │      something not yet decided.                                         │
     │    - opposed: a formal objection, petition, or councillor objecting.    │
     │    - unknown: no note mentions the council or the community. Claude     │
     │      must not infer sentiment from owner enthusiasm ("Owner very keen"  │
     │      is about the owner, not the community).                            │
     │ 2. Code checks the quote is verbatim in a note. If more than one        │
     │    sentiment note exists, the latest dated one wins. Today no site has  │
     │    two.                                                                 │
     │ 3. Code maps supportive → 5, neutral → 3, opposed → 0, unknown → 0      │
     │    (best case 5). C4 points = 10 × tier / 5.                            │

     │                                                                         │
     │ Formula check (S-003): C1 4.6 MW → 5 → 30 × 5/5 = 30. C2 0.8 km → 5 →   │

⏺ Updated plan
  ⎿  /plan to preview

⏺ The quote-matching check stays since it verifies exact quotes and identifies
  source dates, while your owner_status pattern is now applied both in the
  prompt (labeling which notes hold council/community reactions) and as a code
  cross-check that flags mismatches; Claude will still read all notes, since
  other facts like S-013's area figure live outside the sentiment notes.

  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/deep-prancing-teacup.md · /plan to edit
     Part 2: working slice (rank.py + Claude notes extraction + README)

     Context

     Take-home "Five sites by Thursday" (repo
     ~/Downloads/assignment-venture-engineer, private GitHub
     prab-gupta/five-sites-by-thursday). QUESTIONS.md is committed first (done).
     Part 2 needs one command that loads data/, has Claude read the field notes
     into quoted signals, scores in code against the sponsor rubric, ranks, and
     shows why each site scored what it did. Plus a short README.md. The
     AI-step design is already reviewed and committed at
     docs/plans/2026-09-25-001-feat-notes-extraction-plan.md. This plan adds the
     data layer, scoring and README around it. The phone page (Part 3) is out
     of scope here.

     Tech stack

     - Python 3, one file (rank.py). Standard library only for data: json, csv,
       argparse, pathlib. No pandas, no database, no framework, because 40
       records fit in a list.
     - anthropic SDK 0.115.1 (already installed) for the one Claude call per
       site. No agent framework.
     - Outputs: terminal table and out/results.json. Part 3 later adds static
       HTML generated by the same script, deployed to Vercel.
     - python-dotenv (the one new dependency, pinned in requirements.txt).
       load_dotenv(override=True) makes the .env key win over the shell's
       personal key.

     Settled decisions (from QUESTIONS.md and the reviews, not reopened)

     - Rubric weights and tiers exactly as in the brief. No averaging.
     - Missing = 0, marked unknown. Also show a best-case score (missing = 5)
       next to it. Rank by the missing = 0 score.
     - Vendor headroom only when gridmap has no figure, at the low end of its
       band: est × (1 − band_pct/100), flagged "unverified".
     - Kill rule applies if landreg says protected_area: true or a note says so
       (source: registry | note).
     - Nordholm headroom ÷ 1000 in the script.
     - Owner status (C3) comes from the typed owner_status field on the latest
       note. Claude's reading is stored next to it, and the page shows "check"
       if the two differ.

     Files

     requirements.txt          # anthropic, python-dotenv
     rank.py                   # the only script: load → fix → extract (cached) 
     → score → print/write
     prompts/extract_notes.md  # Claude prompt (no rubric numbers)
     out/extractions.json      # committed cache of Claude output (reproducible 
     without a key)
     out/results.json          # full per-site breakdown (source for Part 3 
     page)
     eval/gold_notes.json      # hand labels for the 18 distinct note texts
     EVAL.md                   # extraction check result
     README.md

     rank.py — sections in order

     1. Load + env. load_dotenv(override=True), then load sites.json and
     fetch_log.csv.

     2. Data fixes (one function, one comment per trap)
     - Nordholm: headroom / 1000. One line, no extra guard. README notes: remove
       it once Jonas's feed converts, or it will divide twice.
     - Why gridmap is empty, read from fetch_log:
       - 503 upstream timeout (S-004, 011, 015, 022, 026, 029, 035, 038):
         headroom and distance are unknown. Vendor low-band applies to C1. C2 is
         unknown = 0.
       - 200 "no substation within search radius (10 km)" (S-008, 019): a real
         negative. C1 = 0 and C2 = 0, noted as "no substation within 10 km", not
         unknown.
     - Vendor: ignored completely wherever gridmap has a figure (not stored, not
       shown). Only used for the 8 timeout sites, at the low end of its band.
     - Duplicate: S-017 and S-031 share parcel SV-2291, so merge them into one
       site "S-017/S-031" with all notes combined. The merged site resolves to
       LOI signed (S-031's 09-08 note) and neutral sentiment.
     - Landreg 404 (S-024, S-036): area, flood and protected are unknown. Not
       killed, since that's unknown, not protected.

     3. Claude extraction. Per
     docs/plans/2026-09-25-001-feat-notes-extraction-plan.md:
     - One forced-tool-call per site. MODEL env var, default claude-opus-5.
     - The schema is owner_status, sentiment, area_override_m2, protected_area,
       each {value, quote}, plus caveats[{detail, quote}].
     - Cache in out/extractions.json, keyed by site_id and written after each
       site. --refresh re-calls the API. S-024 has no notes, so it gets all
       unknowns with no API call.
     - Code checks every quote is a verbatim substring of a note (after
       whitespace normalisation). If it isn't, the value becomes unknown and the
       rejected quote is recorded. The note that contains the quote gives the
       date.
     - Latest note wins per signal.
     - Area override only if the note is newer than landreg record_date: S-013
       becomes 700 m².
     - Note-sourced protected flag triggers the kill with source: note: S-009,
       labelled "needs checking".

     4. Rubric in code (a dict of tiers, the one place that holds the numbers)
     - C1 headroom: ≥ 4 → 5, ≥ 2 → 3, ≥ 1 → 1, else 0. Boundary: exactly 2.0
       counts as "2–4", matching how "≥ 4" is written. Stated in the README.
     - C2 distance: ≤ 1 → 5, ≤ 3 → 3, ≤ 6 → 1, else 0.
     - C3 owner: loi_signed 5, in_talks 3, not_contacted 1, refused 0.
     - C4 sentiment: supportive 5, neutral 3, opposed 0.
     - C5 flood: none 5, low 4, medium 2, high 0.
     - C6 area: ≥ 1200 → 5, ≥ 800 → 2, else 0.
     - Score = Σ weight × tier / 5. Best case = the same sum with unknown tiers
       set to 5.
     - Kill: the site is excluded with its reason, source and quote.
     - Per criterion, each site keeps: tier, raw value, source (gridmap | 
       vendor-lowband | landreg | note | typed-field | unknown), and quote if
       any. That per-criterion record is the "why".
     - Ranking: sort by score, then fewer unknowns. Top 5 are the shortlist.

     Rubric input map (every input exists; no gaps)

     #: C1
     Weight: 30
     Raw input: gridmap.headroom
     Conversion before tiering: Nordholm ÷ 1000 (kW to MW). Timeout sites:
     vendor ×
     (1 − band/100). "No substation": tier 0
     If missing: 0, unknown
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: C2
     Weight: 15
     Raw input: gridmap.substation_distance_km
     Conversion before tiering: none (already km). "No substation": tier 0
     If missing: 0, unknown (timeout sites)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: C3
     Weight: 20
     Raw input: typed owner_status on the latest note
     Conversion before tiering: S-017/S-031 merged first
     If missing: 0, unknown (S-024, S-036)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: C4
     Weight: 10
     Raw input: Claude sentiment from the notes
     Conversion before tiering: quote must match a note
     If missing: 0, unknown (12 sites)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: C5
     Weight: 15
     Raw input: landreg.flood_zone
     Conversion before tiering: none
     If missing: 0, unknown (S-024, S-036)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: C6
     Weight: 10
     Raw input: landreg.area_m2
     Conversion before tiering: replaced by Claude area_override_m2 if the note
     is
     newer than record_date (S-013 becomes 700)
     If missing: 0, unknown
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     #: Kill
     Weight: —
     Raw input: landreg.protected_area or Claude protected_area
     Conversion before tiering: —
     If missing: not killed

     C4 community sentiment: how it's derived
     1. Claude gets the site's notes and the definitions below (no points), and
        returns sentiment: {value, quote}.
        - supportive: the council, a councillor, a council committee, or a local
          group formally backs the project.
        - neutral: no position, indifferent, or support conditional on something
          not yet decided.
        - opposed: a formal objection, petition, or councillor objecting.
        - unknown: no note mentions the council or the community. Claude must
          not infer sentiment from owner enthusiasm ("Owner very keen" is about
          the owner, not the community).
        - Pattern hint in the prompt (QUESTIONS.md Q9): notes with an empty
          owner_status are the ones that record council/community reactions.
          Each note is sent tagged with its owner_status (or "none"), so Claude
          can use that pattern. Claude still reads every note, because area and
          protected facts also sit in owner notes (S-013) or in non-sentiment
          notes (S-009 ecologist, S-036 drive-by).
     2. Code checks the quote is verbatim in a note. This is the brief's "exact
        quote, no guessing" rule made enforceable: a paraphrased or invented
        quote gets caught by one substring test. It also tells code which note
        the quote came from, and so its date. Pattern cross-check: if a
        sentiment quote comes from a note that has an owner_status, flag it
        "check", because it breaks the pattern. If more than one sentiment note
        exists, the latest dated one wins. Today no site has two.
     3. Code maps supportive → 5, neutral → 3, opposed → 0, unknown → 0 (best
        case 5). C4 points = 10 × tier / 5.

     What this gives on the real data (7 phrasings):
     - supportive: "Local energy co-op wrote to the council backing the project"
       (7 sites), "Council planning committee minutes record support" (5),
       "ward councillor … openly in favour" (5)
     - neutral: "Neighbours we spoke to were indifferent" (5), "Council has no
       formal position yet; … depends on the noise report" (4)
     - opposed: S-030 residents' petition, S-040 councillor fire-safety
       objections
     - unknown: 12 sites with no council or community note

     Formula check (S-003): C1 4.6 MW → 5 → 30 × 5/5 = 30. C2 0.8 km → 5 → 15.
     C3 LOI → 5 → 20. C4 supportive → 5 → 10. C5 low → 4 → 15 × 4/5 = 12. C6
     2,100 m² → 5 → 10. Total 97. Assert S-003 = 97 in rank.py so the formula is
     checked on every run.

     5. Output
     - Terminal: a ranked table with score, best case, confidence ("n/6 known"),
       flags, and the excluded list with reasons.
     - python3 rank.py S-013 prints one site's full breakdown: each criterion
       with value, source, tier, points and quote. This is the "why" for any
       site.
     - Writes out/results.json for the Part 3 page.
     - Asserts on every run:
       - 39 sites after the merge
       - S-003 scores 97 (formula check)
       - S-020 is refused
       - S-013 area is 700
       - S-009 is excluded with source note
       - S-033 is excluded with source registry

     6. --check. Load eval/gold_notes.json (18 distinct texts labelled by hand),
     expand the labels to every site by matching note text, and resolve with
     the same latest-note logic. Compare against Claude's output per signal,
     then print agreement and each mismatch with its quote. The result goes into
     EVAL.md.

     README.md (short)

     - Setup: pip install -r requirements.txt, put the key in .env.
     - Run: python3 rank.py (cached, no key needed), python3 rank.py --refresh
       (needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY), python3 rank.py S-0xx, python3 rank.py 
       --check.
     - Decisions: a list with one line each, linking to QUESTIONS.md for the
       reasoning. Covers:
       - the traps and how each is handled
       - Claude reads, code scores
       - missing = 0 plus best case
       - vendor low-band
       - the tier boundary choice
       - the merged duplicate
     - Time spent: filled in at the end.

     Commits (in order, each reviewed by user)

     1. Data layer + rubric scoring (runs with no Claude yet: notes-based
        criteria unknown).
     2. Prompt + extraction + quote checks + cache (run live once, commit
        cache).
     3. Gold labels + --check + EVAL.md.
     4. README.

     Verification

     - python3 rank.py from a clean clone works without a key (cache committed)
       and prints the ranking. A second run makes 0 API calls.
     - All asserts pass.
     - python3 rank.py S-013 shows area 700 from a note with its quote. python3 
       rank.py S-009 shows "excluded — note, needs checking".
     - python3 rank.py --check agreement is reported in EVAL.md, and every
       mismatch is explained.
     - grep -r sk-ant over the repo and git log -p finds nothing.
     - Sanity check against the data: expected top five are roughly S-003, S-018
       (Nordholm 4.6 MW), S-017/S-031, S-027, S-014/S-032. The code decides; if
       it differs, explain why in the README.

      110 +def extract(client, site):                                    
      111 +    """One call per site. Returns Claude's raw JSON; nothing i
          +s trusted until verify()."""                                  
      112 +    notes = sorted(site["field_notes"], key=lambda n: n["date"
          +])                                                            
      113 +    lines = [f"[{n['date']}] (owner_status: {n.get('owner_stat
          +us') or 'none'}) {n['text']}" for n in notes]                 
      114 +    resp = client.messages.create(                            
      115 +        model=MODEL, max_tokens=16000, system=PROMPT,         
      116 +        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "\n".join(lines)
          +}],

          +ma": SCHEMA}},                                                
      118 +    )                                                         
      119 +    if resp.stop_reason != "end_turn":                        
      120 +        raise RuntimeError(f"{site['site_id']}: stop_reason {r
          +esp.stop_reason}")                                            
      121 +    return json.loads(next(b.text for b in resp.content if b.t
          +ype == "text"))

      124 +def extractions(sites, refresh):                              
      125 +    """Cached per site in out/extractions.json; only missing s
          +ites hit the API."""                                          
      126 +    path = OUT / "extractions.json"                           
      127 +    cache = {} if refresh or not path.exists() else json.loads
          +(path.read_text())                                            
      128 +    client = None                                             
      129 +    for s in sites:                                           
      130 +        if s["site_id"] in cache or not s["field_notes"]:

      144 +def verify(raw, notes):

      146 +    def source(quote):                                        
      147 +        return next((n for n in notes if quote and norm(quote)
          + in norm(n["text"])), None)                                   
      148 +

      150 +    for key in ("owner_status", "sentiment", "area_m2", "prote
          +cted_area"):

      154 +        n = source(sig["quote"])                              
      155 +        if not n:

      159 +                    "check": key == "sentiment" and bool(n.get
          +("owner_status"))}

          +veats", []) if not source(c["quote"])]                        
      162 +    out["rejected"] = rejected                                
      163 +    return out

      171 +def check(sites, raw):                                        
      172 +    """Compare Claude's verified signals with hand labels (eva
          +l/gold_notes.json, one per distinct note)."""                 
      173 +    gold = json.loads((ROOT / "eval/gold_notes.json").read_tex
          +t())                                                          
      174 +    agree = {f: 0 for f in FIELDS}                            
      175 +    mismatches, rejected, compared = [], [], 0                
      176 +    for s in sites:                                           
      177 +        if not s["field_notes"]:                              
      178 +            continue                                          
      179 +        compared += 1                                         
      180 +        got = verify(raw.get(s["site_id"], {}), s["field_notes
          +"])                                                           
      181 +        rejected += [(s["site_id"], r) for r in got["rejected"
          +]]                                                            
      182 +        for f in FIELDS:                                      
      183 +            labelled = [(n["date"], gold[n["text"]][f]) for n 
          +in s["field_notes"]                                           
      184 +                        if gold[n["text"]][f] not in ("unknown
          +", None)]                                                     
      185 +            want = max(labelled)[1] if labelled else "unknown"
      186 +            have = got.get(f, {}).get("value", "unknown")     
      187 +            if want == have:                                  
      188 +                agree[f] += 1                                 
      189 +            else:                                             
      190 +                mismatches.append((s["site_id"], f, want, have
          +, got.get(f, {}).get("quote")))                               
      191 +    print(f"Sites compared: {compared} (S-024 has no notes)\n"
          +)                                                             
      192 +    print("| Signal | Agree |\n|---|---|")                    
      193 +    for f in FIELDS:                                          
      194 +        print(f"| {f} | {agree[f]}/{compared} |")             
      195 +    print(f"\nMismatches: {len(mismatches) or 'none'}")       
      196 +    for m in mismatches:                                      
      197 +        print(f"- {m[0]} {m[1]}: hand label `{m[2]}`, Claude `
          +{m[3]}`" + (f' — "{m[4]}"' if m[4] else ""))                  
      198 +    print(f"\nQuotes rejected (not verbatim in the notes): {le
          +n(rejected) or 'none'}")                                      
      199 +    for sid, r in rejected:                                   
      200 +        print(f"- {sid} {r['field']}: \"{r['quote']}\"")

  Change from the plan: I used structured output (a JSON schema on the response)
  instead of a forced tool call. It's simpler, it guarantees valid JSON, and a
  forced tool call can clash with thinking on Opus 5. I didn't add the API's
  automatic "fallback model" option. These notes are harmless text, so a refusal
  is unlikely; if one happens, the run stops with an error.

  I'm committing straight to main, as we have all along, because the graders
  read the history.

  These are judgment calls, so decide them yourself:
  - #7 "depends on the noise report": I put neutral. You could argue it's
    conditional support.

⏺ All three changes are in and tested locally. The top five and their scores
  didn't change, and nothing is committed.

  1. Best case now says what it means
  - The ranking table column is now best*, with this footnote: "Assumes missing
    criteria receive maximum points. Existing values and estimates stay
    unchanged."
  - Per-site output shows the same sentence, and results.json gets a new
    best_case_means field. The best_case field and its calculation are
    unchanged.
  - So S-011's 88 only assumes a perfect distance score. It doesn't assume a
    better vendor headroom figure.

  2. Source dates and a warning when the two headroom figures disagree
  - Official grid rows now show as of <date>. S-007 and S-021 show as of 
    2026-03-31.
  - A new check, headroom_conflict(), compares the official figure with the
    vendor's central estimate. If they land in different rubric tiers, the site
    gets a warning with both figures and dates, a note that the vendor model is
    unvalidated and needs checking with the grid operator, and "Score uses the
    official figure."
  - Scores don't change, and no site IDs are hardcoded.
  - The warning appears in the per-site output, in the table's flags column, and
    in results.json flags. Nordholm is already converted to MW before the
    comparison.
  - This reverses your earlier call to hide the vendor figure when official data
    exists. It's still never used for scoring, only for this warning. Say if
    you want it hidden again.

  3. Unsupported noise-report claims removed
  - I corrected four sites, not two. S-016 ("not yet been decided") and S-021
    ("pending") made the same unsupported claim as S-010 and S-027. All four now
    read: "Council support depends on the noise report; confirm the report's
    status and outcome."
  - The corrections live in a new file, eval/caveat_corrections.json, with
    Claude's original wording and the reason for each. out/extractions.json
    stays pure model output.
  - The output labels each one [corrected by hand; Claude wrote: …]. No API
    calls were made.
  - The prompt got one extra sentence: a caveat restates only what a note says,
    and must not infer missing facts, such as whether a report exists, is
    pending or has been delivered.

  N8N analogy:
  - The conflict check is an IF node after the grid data: when the two figures
    fall in different tiers, it adds a warning to the flags field, and the score
    path is untouched.
  - The corrections file is a Set node that overrides one field after the AI
    node and records what the AI said. It doesn't rewrite the AI node's stored
    output.

❯ # Caldera — Style Reference                                                   
  > forge fire on warm limestone. The canvas is raw warm plaster, and every     
  orange element reads as glowing embers pressed into the surface.              
                                                                                
  **Theme:** light                                                              
                                                                                
  Source measurements are normalized; roles and recommendations are             
  interpreted. Font summary lists are independent, not paired by position. HTML 
  examples are reconstructions, not source components.                          
                                                                                
  Caldera runs on a warm limestone canvas flooded with molten orange. The       
  interface is flat and unshadowed, letting ultrabold compressed type at        
  near-architectural scale (up to 189px) carry all structural weight. A single  
  vivid orange (#fc5000) acts as the only aggressive chromatic accent against   
  monochrome warm grays, with a violet plasma reserved for the hero halftone    
  and a sulfur yellow for tags. The visual language is volcanic: condensed      
  heavy letterforms, halftone dot patterns, 40px radii on cards and buttons,    
  and 800px pill controls — heat contained within a soft, paper-like surface.   
                                                                                
  ## Tokens — Colors                                                            
                                                                                
  | Name | Value | Token | Role |                                               
  |------|-------|-------|------|                                               
  | Ember | `#fc5000` | `--color-ember` | Primary action buttons, featured stat 
  cards, key visual highlights — the only aggressive chromatic accent; its      
  vivid saturation against warm grays creates urgency without needing           
  supporting decorative weight |                                                
  | Plasma Violet | `#524ae9` | `--color-plasma-violet` | Hero halftone         
  gradient base, single secondary card surface — appears almost exclusively in  
  the hero dot pattern and one standout card; never used for controls |         
  | Sulfur | `#f5f28e` | `--color-sulfur` | Tag and category badge backgrounds, 
  small highlight washes — the soft yellow that labels blog post categories     
  and program announcements |                                                   
  | Limestone | `#f7f6f2` | `--color-limestone` | Card surfaces, content block  
  backgrounds, secondary button fills — the lighter warm off-white that lifts   
  elements off the page canvas |                                                
  | Pumice | `#e2e2df` | `--color-pumice` | Page canvas, dominant background —  
  the warm medium-gray that grounds every section; slightly darker than card    
  surfaces to create subtle figure/ground separation without shadows |          
  | Obsidian | `#070607` | `--color-obsidian` | Primary text, headings, link    
  text, button borders — near-black with a barely-warm cast matching the canvas 
  warmth |                                                                      
  | Chalk | `#ffffff` | `--color-chalk` | Dark-section text, input text on dark 
  backgrounds, high-contrast overlays — pure white used only where maximum      
  contrast against dark surfaces is required |                                  
                                                                                
  ## Tokens — Typography                                                        
                                                                                
  #                                                                             
  ──── (275 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  ing-92: 92px;

    --card-padding: 40px;                                                       
    --element-gap: 16px;                                                        
                                                                                
    /* Border Radius */                                                         
    --radius-2xl: 16px;                                                         
    --radius-2xl-2: 20px;                                                       
    --radius-3xl: 24px;                                                         
    --radius-3xl-2: 32px;                                                       
    --radius-3xl-3: 40px;                                                       
    --radius-full: 100px;                                                       
    --radius-full-2: 800px;                                                     
                                                                                
    /* Named Radii */                                                           
    --radius-cards: 40px;                                                       
    --radius-pills: 800px;                                                      
    --radius-small: 16px;                                                       
    --radius-inputs: 100px;                                                     
    --radius-medium: 20px;

    /* Surfaces */                                                              
    --surface-pumice-canvas: #e2e2df;

⏺ The phone page is built and tested locally. Nothing is committed or deployed.

  Test it yourself:
  cd ~/Downloads/assignment-venture-engineer
  python3 page.py
  open site/index.html
  To see the phone view in Chrome, open DevTools (Cmd+Opt+I), then turn on
  device mode (Cmd+Shift+M) and pick an iPhone.

  What's on the page, top to bottom:
  1. Headline block: violet-to-orange, with halftone dots in the corner only.
     "Bring these five sites on Thursday", how they were picked, and the data
     date.
  2. The five cards: rank, name, orange score pill, yellow condition tag
     (Kessby, Varne, Orlund), a plain-language reason, and a "Why this score +"
     panel. The panel shows each criterion with points, source with date, and
     the exact quote.
  3. Check before or during the meeting: Kessby's 12% revenue share, Varne's
     noise report, Orlund's unsigned owner agreement, each with its quote.
  4. Why not these:
     - S-009: would score 100, possible nature reserve
     - S-013: about 700 m² left
     - S-024: 45 now, 100 only if everything checks out
  5. How sure we are: "6/6 known" doesn't mean feasible. Grid figures are
     quarterly, and letters of intent are non-binding.
  6. What to check next: four steps.

  Checks I ran

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Audit: almost nothing to cut. There's one real finding.

  yagni: the rubric weights are copied into page.py, and the page recomputes 
  points from that copy (page.py:17, :64). A second copy of the sponsor's 
  numbers can silently drift from rank.py. Replacement: rank.py writes each 
  criterion's points and weight into results.json, and page.py just displays 
  them. [page.py:17,64]
  net: -3 lines, -0 deps possible.

  Everything else earns its place:
  - python-dotenv is there because you asked for it.
  - MODEL is one line, and the brief says any model is fine.
  - The stored Claude owner value is the "store both" decision you made.
  - page.py has its own labels on purpose: plain words for Maren.

  ---

  Where and when Claude is called

  N8N analogy: extract() is the AI node. extractions() is an IF node in front of
  it: "already in the cache? skip the AI node".

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Where      │                     What it does                      │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ rank.py:115     │ The only API call (client.messages.create, line 119). │
  │ extract()       │  One call reads one site's notes.                     │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ Loops over sites and calls extract() only if the site │
  │ rank.py:129     │  isn't already in out/extractions.json and has notes  │
  │ extractions()   │ (line 135). The client is created only when needed    │
  │                 │ (line 137).                                           │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ rank.py:340     │ Runs extractions() on every command: the plain run,   │
  │ main()          │ S-013, and --check.                                   │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ rank.py:332     │ Loads .env, so the Limineer key wins over your        │
  │                 │ shell's own key.                                      │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ page.py         │ Never calls Claude. It only reads results.json.       │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  When a call actually happens
  - Normal runs: zero calls. All 38 sites are cached. S-024 has no notes, so
    it's never sent.
  - A site is missing from the cache: one call for that site.
  - --refresh: the cache is emptied, so 38 calls, about 5 minutes.

  ---

  How to test the calls

  1. Cheapest test: re-read one site (one call).
  cp out/extractions.json /tmp/ext.bak
  python3 -c "import json;p='out/extractions.json';d=json.load(open(p));d.pop('S
  -013');json.dump(d,open(p,'w'),indent=2,sort_keys=True)"
  .venv/bin/python rank.py S-013
     - Expected: the line reading notes: S-013, then the same breakdown as
       before: area 700 with its quote.
     - Restore after: cp /tmp/ext.bak out/extractions.json
  2. Prove the check catches a bad quote (no API call). Edit one quote in the
     cache to text that isn't in the notes. rank.py --check then reports it
     under "Quotes rejected", and that signal becomes unknown. Restore from the
     backup afterwards.
  3. Full re-read: --refresh, 38 calls. Back up first with the same cp command.
     One side effect: my 4 hand corrections to the noise-report caveats are
     matched on Claude's old wording. After a refresh they won't match anymore,
     so they won't apply. With the updated prompt Claude should no longer write
     that claim, but read the caveats afterwards to check.

  Next: run test 1. It costs one API call and takes about 10 seconds.

❯ how do i test with real api calls walk me step by step                        

⏺ You'll test with real API calls in two stages. First one site (1 call, about
  10 seconds), then optionally all 38 sites (about 5 minutes). You can undo
  everything, because the first step backs up the cache.

  Bubble analogy: out/extractions.json works like a saved API Connector
  response. Normally the app reuses the saved response. The tests below delete
  part of it, so the app has to make the real call again.

  ---

  Part A: one real call (S-013)

  1. Go to the project
  cd ~/Downloads/assignment-venture-engineer

  2. Back up Claude's saved answers
  cp out/extractions.json /tmp/extractions.backup.json

  3. Check the right key is used (it prints only the last 4 characters)
  .venv/bin/python -c "from dotenv import load_dotenv;import 
  os;load_dotenv('.env',override=True);print('using key 
  ...'+os.environ['ANTHROPIC_API_KEY'][-4:])"
  tail -c 5 .env
  - Expected: both lines end with the same 4 characters. That means the Limineer
    key from .env wins over your personal key in the shell.

  4. Remove S-013 from the saved answers
  python3 -c "import json;p='out/extractions.json';d=json.load(open(p));d.pop('S
  -013');json.dump(d,open(p,'w'),indent=2,sort_keys=True)"

  5. Run it. This makes the real API call.
  .venv/bin/python rank.py S-013
  - Expected: first the line reading notes: S-013, which is the live call. Then
    the breakdown, which should match what you saw before:
    - owner in talks
    - council supportive
    - area 700 from the note, with the quote roughly 700 m² remains
    - the "approximate: confirm size" flag

  6. Look at exactly what Claude returned
  python3 -c "import json;print(json.dumps(json.load(open('out/extractions.json'
  ))['S-013'],indent=2,ensure_ascii=False))"
  - Check: every signal has a value and a quote copied word for word from the
    notes. protected_area should be unknown with an empty quote.

  7. Compare with the old answer
  diff <(python3 -c "import json;print(json.dumps(json.load(open('/tmp/extractio
  ns.backup.json'))['S-013'],indent=2,ensure_ascii=False))") <(python3 -c 
  "import json;print(json.dumps(json.load(open('out/extractions.json'))['S-013']
  ,indent=2,ensure_ascii=False))")
  - Expected: the values and quotes are the same. The caveat's wording (detail)
    may differ, because Claude phrases things a bit differently each run. That's
    fine: the check only uses values and quotes.

  8. Re-run the full check
  .venv/bin/python rank.py --check
  - Expected: 38/38 on all four signals, and "Quotes rejected: none".

  Part A is done: one real call, verified against your hand labels.

  ---

  Part B (optional): re-read all 38 sites

  9. Re-read everything (38 calls, about 5 minutes)
  .venv/bin/python rank.py --refresh
  You'll see reading notes: S-001 … S-040 scroll by, then the ranking.

  10. Check the results
  .venv/bin/python rank.py --check
  .venv/bin/python rank.py | head -7
  - Expected: 38/38 and the same top five (97, 97, 96, 93, 86).
  - If anything differs: that's a real finding. --check names the site, the
    signal and the quote. Paste it to me.

  11. Check the noise-report caveats yourself
  for s in S-010 S-016 S-021 S-027; do .venv/bin/python rank.py $s | grep 
  caveat; done
  - Why: my 4 hand corrections match Claude's old wording, so after a refresh
    they no longer apply.
  - What to look for: the new prompt tells Claude not to invent a report's
    status. None of these lines should say "pending", "not yet delivered" or
    "not decided". If one does, tell me and I'll add a correction.

  ---

  Undo at any time

  cp /tmp/extractions.backup.json out/extractions.json
  .venv/bin/python rank.py > /dev/null && python3 page.py

  Next: do Part A step 1 to step 5, then paste the rank.py S-013 output here.

  What changed
  1. --refresh SITE …: you can re-read chosen sites with real API calls, e.g.
     rank.py --refresh S-013. A plain --refresh still re-reads all 38. No more
     editing JSON by hand.
  2. The corrections file is gone. I re-read S-010, S-016, S-021 and S-027 with
     the fixed prompt (4 real calls).
     - None of them claims the report's status anymore. S-010 now says
       "conditional on the noise report", and S-016 and S-021 no longer have a
       caveat.
     - All 16 signals are unchanged.
     - So eval/caveat_corrections.json and its code are deleted. The cause was
       fixed in the prompt instead of patched afterwards.
  3. Call counter: every run ends with Claude calls this run: N (M from cache 
     out/extractions.json). The refresh run showed 4 (34 from cache), and normal
     runs show 0 (38 from cache).
  4. README:
     - "Run it" now covers the four commands, the call-count line, and why no
       key is needed.
     - page.py never calls Claude.
     - A new section, "Calling Claude for real", covers .env, MODEL, the three
       --refresh forms, then --check, and when a call actually happens.
     - The caveat row in the decisions table now says "fixed at the source".

  3. Why not the obvious ones:
     - Esk Mill: would score 100, possible reserve
     - Rook Street: about 700 m² left
     - Pellick Lane: strong grid, nothing else checked
     - plus one line on the quicker method: Jonas's approach would have brought
       Esk Mill and Ulven Works. Ulven is 1.7 MW, not 1,700, because the
       kilowatts weren't converted.
  4. How sure we are: every rubric item filled doesn't mean feasible. Grid
     figures are official from June, letters of intent are non-binding, and
     missing values scored zero.
  5. What I'd check next: four items, including confirming Nordholm's kilowatts,
     since that affects Grelle.

  For you to check
  - I checked every figure against results.json, but read it for tone. It's
    written as you, to Maren.
  - The Ulven Works line isn't on the page. If you want the page and the note to
    match exactly, I can add it to the page's "Why not these" section, or drop
    it from the note.
  - "page URL to be added" stays until it's deployed.

  Next: open NOTE.md and tell me what to change.

⏺ The score pill is now patina green #1faa6b, like the green of weathered
  copper.

  Why this green:
  - It fits the palette. A mineral green sits well next to the warm grey, the
    orange and the violet, where a bright "app" green would clash.
  - Readable: the dark score text on it has a contrast ratio of 6.8:1, which
    passes the accessibility standard (WCAG AA) for small text like "/100".
  - Still stands out against the off-white card.
  - Meaning: green reads as "recommended", which suits the top-five scores.
    Orange stays for the hero.

  It's defined once as --patina in page.py, so it's easy to change.
  site/index.html is regenerated, and nothing is committed.

  Next: refresh the page on your phone and tell me the next change.
